[[Calls and calls at ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Even though it is clearly proven that it is different, it is ultimately still about counter-businesses, and one tries to establish a conflict there by saying: That doesn't mean anything, and the whole thing isn't being handled honestly either! Now for me it is the question – and I would like to say it quite objectively: What really is the value of a counter-business for an observer from outside? – In my assessment and in my very concrete experience as a long-standing former Minister of Defence, there are two main reasons. The first is that very often new business areas are opened up with the counter-businesses, that Austrian companies can receive orders, enter high technology, in fields where there has not been this, because they directly supply or otherwise. This is clearly recognizable for everyone. It is more difficult to see when it comes to securing a certain volume of orders. This is not so clear and not so easily recognizable, because some of the companies have already made sales, some have had contracts et cetera. But this is exactly the point that anyone who has or is active in the private sector can tell you how important it is. I come from the private sector, I was also the sales manager of a large, competitive company for years, and I know that even if you have a turnover in a certain volume with a large trading company every year, each year it is extremely difficult to return to such a volume. You have to use everything and there for every order to fight any action or whatever et cetera. In such cases counter-business security is an extremely valuable moment! I can only tell you from my own experience how often I have experienced it in Austria – even in the time when I was no longer Minister of Defense, but in my capacity as ÖAAB-Obmann somewhere – that people told me: Thank God, we received this commission at the time, that was in the context of counter-businesses, and we still have a certain guarantee for years to come that this volume of business will be achieved! That is why, ladies and gentlemen, you should consider this procedure. I believe that opposition policy should be tough, but it should not be aimed at simply discriminating and scandalizing an entire area. Indeed, there is no reason for scandalisation and discrimination! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In this sense, I would also like to express my personal compliment to Mr Bartenstein on the way in which he is pursuing and reviewing the conduct of counter-actions in a serious and conscientious manner. I have to say that my compliment for what I can say here is quite competent: It's first-class! If you know that in the entire assessment process not only international companies such as Ernst & Young and so on are involved, but also the Chamber of Commerce, the Chamber of Workers and several others are involved, then you should also judge this differently.
